Exposition Pattern: Kubernetes vs Grafana\\n\\n### Overview\\n\\nBoth Kubernetes and Grafana implement the Prometheus metrics exposition pattern using the prometheus/client_golang library, but they organize their metrics infrastructure differently based on their architectural needs.\\n\\n### Kubernetes Architecture\\n\\n**Registry Management (legacyregistry):**\\nKubernetes uses a centralized metrics registry pattern in `staging/src/k8s.io/component-base/metrics/legacyregistry/registry.go`. The key components are:\\n\\n- **defaultRegistry**: A single global `metrics.NewKubeRegistry()` that serves as the central metrics repository\\n- **DefaultGatherer**: Exposes the gatherer interface for collecting metrics\\n- **Handler() and HandlerWithReset()**: Factory functions that return HTTP handlers instrumented with `promhttp.InstrumentMetricHandler()`\\n- **MustRegister**: Global function that registers metrics with the default registry\\n\\nThe registry automatically includes Go runtime and process metrics via collectors registered in the init() function (line 54-56), and includes Kubernetes-specific prometheus extensions.\\n\\n**Metrics Routes (staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/routes/metrics.go):**\\nKubernetes exposes metrics via route installation classes:\\n\\n- **DefaultMetrics**: Installs `/metrics` endpoint using `legacyregistry.Handler()`\\n- **MetricsWithReset**: Installs `/metrics` endpoint with DELETE method support via `legacyregistry.HandlerWithReset()`\\n- **register() function**: Explicitly registers multiple metric collectors:\\n  - apimetrics.Register()\\n  - cachermetrics.Register()\\n  - etcd3metrics.Register()\\n  - flowcontrolmetrics.Register()\\n  - peerproxymetrics.Register()\\n  - handlersmetrics.Register()\\n\\n**Distributed Metric Definition Pattern:**\\nKubernetes defines metrics across many modules using a consistent pattern. Example from `staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/audit/metrics.go`:\\n\\n- Metrics are defined as package-level variables (eventCounter, errorCounter, levelCounter, ApiserverAuditDroppedCounter)\\n- Each metric uses `metrics.NewCounter()` or `metrics.NewCounterVec()` with metadata (Subsystem, Name, Help, StabilityLevel)\\n- Metrics are registered in init() functions via `legacyregistry.MustRegister()`\\n- StabilityLevel is enforced for metric governance\\n\\n**Metrics Provider Pattern:**\\nKubernetes uses interface-based metric providers for pluggable metrics. In `staging/src/k8s.io/component-base/metrics/prometheus/workqueue/metrics.go`, the `prometheusMetricsProvider` struct implements provider interfaces that allow components like workqueue and leader election to emit metrics without direct dependencies on prometheus.\\n\\n### Grafana Architecture\\n\\n**Metric Definition (pkg/infra/metrics/metrics.go):**\\nGrafana declares all metrics as package-level variables in a single metrics definition file:\\n\\n- **ExporterName constant**: Set to \\\"grafana\\\" as the namespace for metrics\\n- **Metric Variables**: Declared directly (e.g., MInstanceStart, MPageStatus, MApiStatus, MProxyStatus, etc.) with types from prometheus client_golang\\n- **init() function**: Uses `initMetricVars(reg prometheus.Registerer)` to register all metrics at startup\\n- **Custom Initialization**: Some metrics use metricutil.NewCounterVecStartingAtZero() for custom initialization behavior\\n\\n**Service-Based Management (pkg/infra/metrics/service.go):**\\nGrafana uses dependency injection via Wire for metrics management:\\n\\n- **ProvideService**: Returns InternalMetricsService that initializes metrics and handles graphite bridge\\n- **ProvideRegisterer**: Returns `legacyregistry.Registerer()` (wrapping Kubernetes' legacyregistry)\\n- **ProvideGatherer**: Returns a custom `multiRegistry` that combines multiple gatherers\\n- **addPrefixWrapper**: Ensures all metrics get the \\\"grafana_\\\" prefix if not already present\\n- **multiRegistry**: Combines Kubernetes gatherer (k8sGatherer) with prometheus.DefaultGatherer\\n\\n**Separate Instrumentation Server (pkg/server/instrumentation_service.go):**\\nGrafana runs metrics exposition on a separate HTTP server:\\n\\n- **instrumentationService**: A service that runs independently with its own http.Server\\n- **newInstrumentationServer()**: Creates an http.Server with `http.NewServeMux()` and handles `/metrics` using `promhttp.Handler()`\\n- Runs on `:` + cfg.HTTPPort (configurable port)\\n- Independent lifecycle management via BasicService interface\\n\\n**Wire Dependency Injection (pkg/infra/metrics/wireset.go):**\\n- Uses Google Wire for dependency injection\\n- Provides WireSet and WireSetForTest configurations\\n- Integrates with broader application dependency graph in pkg/server/wire.go (line 397)\\n\\n### Key Differences\\n\\n**1. Registry Strategy:**\\n- **Kubernetes**: Distributed metric registration across modules, centralized via legacyregistry\\n- **Grafana**: Centralized metric definition in one file, distributed registration via initMetricVars()\\n\\n**2. HTTP Endpoint Exposure:**\\n- **Kubernetes**: Metrics integrated into main API server routes via route installation classes\\n- **Grafana**: Metrics exposed on separate dedicated HTTP server (separate port)\\n\\n**3. Exposition Pattern: Kubernetes vs Grafana\\n\\n### Overview\\n\\nBoth Kubernetes and Grafana implement the Prometheus metrics exposition pattern using the prometheus/client_golang library, but they organize their metrics infrastructure differently based on their architectural needs.\\n\\n### Kubernetes Architecture\\n\\n**Registry Management (legacyregistry):**\\nKubernetes uses a centralized The key components are:\\n\\n- **defaultRegistry**: A single global `metrics.NewKubeRegistry()` that serves as the central metrics repository\\n- **DefaultGatherer**: Exposes the gatherer interface for collecting metrics\\n- **Handler() and HandlerWithReset()**: Factory functions that return HTTP handlers instrumented with `promhttp.InstrumentMetricHandler()`\\n- **MustRegister**: Global function that registers metrics with the default registry\\n\\nThe registry automatically includes Go runtime and process metrics via collectors registered in the init() function (line 54-56), and includes Kubernetes-specific prometheus extensions.\\n\\n**Metrics Routes (staging/src/k8s.io/apiserver/pkg/server/routes/metrics.go):**\\nKubernetes exposes metrics via route installation classes:\\n\\n- **DefaultMetrics**: Installs `/metrics` endpoint using `legacyregistry.Handler()`\\n- **MetricsWithReset**: Installs `/metrics` endpoint with DELETE method support via `legacyregistry.HandlerWithReset()`\\n- **register() function**: Explicitly registers multiple metric collectors:\\n  - apimetrics.Register()\\n  - cachermetrics.Register()\\n  - etcd3metrics.Register()\\n  - flowcontrolmetrics.Register()\\n  - peerproxymetrics.Register()\\n  - handlersmetrics.Register()\\n\\n**Distributed Metric Definition Pattern:**\\nKubernetes defines metrics across many modules using a consistent pattern. In `staging/src/k8s.io/component-base/metrics/prometheus/workqueue/metrics.go`, the `prometheusMetricsProvider` struct implements provider interfaces that allow components like workqueue and leader election to emit metrics without direct dependencies on prometheus.\\n\\n### Grafana Architecture\\n\\n**Metric Definition (pkg/infra/metrics/metrics.go):**\\nGrafana declares all metrics as package-level variables in a single metrics definition file:\\n\\n- **ExporterName constant**: Set to \\\"grafana\\\" as the namespace for metrics\\n- **Metric Variables**: Declared directly (e.g.,
